Mathlib.GroupTheory.MonoidLocalization.Order

AddLocalization.mkOrderEmbeddingdefMathlib.GroupTheory.MonoidLocalization.Order, function or constant
Wording
the order embedding of b (from the name)
English
Let $b$ be an element of $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {s}{x_{3}})}$. Then the order embedding of $b$ is an order embedding of $x$ and the set of additive localizations of $s$.
Formula
$b \in \SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {s}{x_{3}})}\vdash \operatorname{mkOrderEmbedding} (b)\in \mathrm{OrderEmbedding} (x , \mathrm{AddLocalization} (s))$

Mathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent

AddGroup.IsNilpotentclassMathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent, predicate
Wording
G is nilpotent (from the name)
English
Let $G$ be a set. Then we can say that $G$ is nilpotent.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{IsNilpotent} (G)\ \mathsf{prop}$
AddGroup.IsVirtuallyNilpotentdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $G$ be a set. Then $\operatorname{IsVirtuallyNilpotent}$ holds for $G$, if there exists an additive subgroup $N$ of $G$, such that $\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {N}{x_{1}})}$ is nilpotent and $N$ is a finite index.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{IsVirtuallyNilpotent} (G):\Leftrightarrow \exists N \in \mathrm{AddSubgroup} (G). \operatorname{IsNilpotent} (\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {N}{x_{1}})})\wedge \operatorname{FiniteIndex} (N)$
AddGroup.nilpotencyClassdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent, function or constant
Wording
the nilpotency class of G (from the docstring)
English
Let $G$ be a set. Then the nilpotency class of $G$ is a natural number defined as $\mathrm{dite}$ applied to the proposition that $G$ is nilpotent, the function that maps $\mathrm{hG}$ to $\operatorname{find}$ applied to $\operatorname{nilpotent}$ applied to $G$ and the function that maps $\mathrm{hG1}$ to $0$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{nilpotencyClass} (G):= \mathrm{dite} (\operatorname{IsNilpotent} (G), \lambda \mathrm{hG} . \operatorname{find} (\operatorname{nilpotent} (G)), \lambda \mathrm{hG1} . 0)\in \Natural$
AddSubgroup.IsAscendingCentralSeriesdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $H$ be a function from natural numbers to additive subgroups of $G$. Then $\operatorname{IsAscendingCentralSeries}$ holds for $H$, if $H$ applied to $0$ is equal to the bottom and for all elements $x_{1}$ of $G$, for all natural numbers $n$, if $\MembershipmemMACRoT {H (n + 1)}{x_{1}}$, then for all elements $g$ of $G$, the bracket of $x_{1}$ and $g$ is an element of $H$ applied to $n$.
Formula
$H \in \Natural \rightarrow \mathrm{AddSubgroup} (G)\vdash \operatorname{IsAscendingCentralSeries} (H):\Leftrightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {H (0)}{\operatorname{bot}}\wedge (\forall x_{1} \in G . \forall n \in \Natural . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {H (n + 1)}{x_{1}}\rightarrow (\forall g \in G . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {H (n)}{\operatorname{bracket} (x_{1} , g)}))$
AddSubgroup.upperCentralSeriesdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent, function or constant
Wording
the upper central series of G and n (from the name)
English
Let $G$ be a set. Let $n$ be a natural number. Then the upper central series of $G$ and $n$ is an additive subgroup of $G$ defined as $\operatorname{fst}$ applied to $\operatorname{upperCentralSeriesAux}$ applied to $G$ and $n$.
Formula
$G \in \mathrm{Set} , n \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{upperCentralSeries} (G , n):= \operatorname{fst} (\operatorname{upperCentralSeriesAux} (G , n))\in \mathrm{AddSubgroup} (G)$
AddSubgroup.upperCentralSeriesStepdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent, function or constant
Wording
the upper central series step of N (from the name)
English
Let $N$ be an additive subgroup of $G$. Then the upper central series step of $N$ is an additive subgroup of $G$.
Formula

AddSubgroup.IsDescendingCentralSeriesdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Nilpotent.LowerCentralSeries, predicate
Wording
H is a descending central series (from the name)
English
Let $H$ be a function from natural numbers to additive subgroups of $G$. Then $H$ is a descending central series, if $H$ applied to $0$ is equal to the top and for all elements $x_{1}$ of $G$, for all natural numbers $n$, if $\MembershipmemMACRoT {H (n)}{x_{1}}$, then for all elements $g$ of $G$, the bracket of $x_{1}$ and $g$ is an element of $H$ applied to $n + 1$.
Formula
$H \in \Natural \rightarrow \mathrm{AddSubgroup} (G)\vdash \operatorname{IsDescendingCentralSeries} (H):\Leftrightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {H (0)}{\operatorname{top}}\wedge (\forall x_{1} \in G . \forall n \in \Natural . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {H (n)}{x_{1}}\rightarrow (\forall g \in G . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {H (n + 1)}{\operatorname{bracket} (x_{1} , g)}))$

Wording

English
Let $H$ be an additive subgroup of $G$. Let $x_{1}$ be a natural number. Then the lower central series of $H$ and $x_{1}$ is an additive subgroup of $G$ defined as $\operatorname{brecOn}$ applied to $x_{1}$ and $\mathrm{AddSubgroup\_\_lowerCentralSeries\_\_\_f}$ applied to $H$.
Formula

Mathlib.GroupTheory.NoncommCoprod

AddHom.noncommCoproddefMathlib.GroupTheory.NoncommCoprod, function or constant
Wording
the noncommutative coproduct of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be an additive homomorphism of $M$ and $P$. Let $g$ be an additive homomorphism of $N$ and $P$ such that for all elements $m$ of $M$, for all elements $n$ of $N$, $\mathrm{AddCommute}$ holds for $f$ applied to $m$ and $g$ applied to $n$. Then the noncommutative coproduct of $f$ and $g$ is an additive homomorphism of $\mathrm{Prod}$ applied to $M$ and $N$ and $P$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{AddHom} (M , P), g \in \mathrm{AddHom} (N , P), \forall m \in M . \forall n \in N . \mathrm{AddCommute} (f (m), g (n))\vdash \operatorname{noncommCoprod} (f , g , \mathrm{comm})\in \mathrm{AddHom} (\mathrm{Prod} (M , N), P)$
AddMonoidHom.noncommCoproddefMathlib.GroupTheory.NoncommCoprod, function or constant
Wording
the noncommutative coproduct of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be an additive monoid homomorphism of $M$ and $P$. Let $g$ be an additive monoid homomorphism of $N$ and $P$ such that for all elements $m$ of $M$, for all elements $n$ of $N$, $\mathrm{AddCommute}$ holds for $f$ applied to $m$ and $g$ applied to $n$. Then the noncommutative coproduct of $f$ and $g$ is an additive monoid homomorphism of $\mathrm{Prod}$ applied to $M$ and $N$ and $P$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (M , P), g \in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (N , P), \forall m \in M . \forall n \in N . \mathrm{AddCommute} (f (m), g (n))\vdash \operatorname{noncommCoprod} (f , g , \mathrm{comm})\in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (\mathrm{Prod} (M , N), P)$

Mathlib.GroupTheory.NoncommPiCoprod

AddMonoidHom.noncommPiCoproddefMathlib.GroupTheory.NoncommPiCoprod, function or constant
Wording
the noncommutative product coproduct of x4 and i2 (from the name)
English
Let $x_{4}$ be a function from elements $i_{2}$ of $x_{1}$ to additive monoid homomorphisms of $N$ applied to $i_{2}$ and $M$ such that the function that maps $i_{1}$ and $j$ to the proposition that for all elements $x_{5}$ of $N$ applied to $i_{1}$, for all elements $y$ of $N$ applied to $j$, $\mathrm{AddCommute}$ holds for $x_{4}$ applied to $i_{1}$ and $x_{5}$ and $x_{4}$ applied to $j$ and $y$ is pairwise. Then the noncommutative product coproduct of $i_{2}$ and $x_{4}$ is an additive monoid homomorphism of the set of functions from elements $i$ of $x_{1}$ to elements of $N$ applied to $i$ and $M$.
Formula
$x_{4} \in x_{1} \rightarrow \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (N (i_{2}), M), \mathrm{Pairwise} (\lambda i_{1} , j . \forall x_{5} \in N (i_{1}). \forall y \in N (j). \mathrm{AddCommute} (x_{4} (i_{1} , x_{5}), x_{4} (j , y)))\vdash \operatorname{noncommPiCoprod} (i_{2} , x_{4} , \mathrm{hcomm})\in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (x_{1} \rightarrow N (i), M)$
AddMonoidHom.noncommPiCoprodEquivdefMathlib.GroupTheory.NoncommPiCoprod, function or constant
Wording
the noncommutative product coproduct equivalence (from the name)
English
The noncommutative product coproduct equivalence is an element of $\EquivMACRoAE {(\SubtypeSet {x_{5}}{\mathrm{Pairwise} (\lambda i . \lambda j . \mathrm{forall} (N (i), \lambda x_{6} . \mathrm{forall} (N (j), \lambda y . \mathrm{AddCommute} (x_{5} (i , x_{6}), x_{5} (j , y)))))})}{\mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (\mathrm{Elem} (x_{1})\rightarrow N (i_{1}), M)}$.
Formula
$\operatorname{noncommPiCoprodEquiv} \in \EquivMACRoAL {(\SubtypeSet {x_{5}}{\mathrm{Pairwise} (\lambda i . \lambda j . \mathrm{forall} (N (i), \lambda x_{6} . \mathrm{forall} (N (j), \lambda y . \mathrm{AddCommute} (x_{5} (i , x_{6}), x_{5} (j , y)))))})}{\mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (\mathrm{Elem} (x_{1})\rightarrow N (i_{1}), M)}$
AddSubgroup.noncommPiCoproddefMathlib.GroupTheory.NoncommPiCoprod, function or constant
Wording
the noncommutative product coproduct of hcomm (from the name)
English
Assume that the function that maps $i_{1}$ and $j$ to the proposition that for all elements $x_{4}$ and $y$ of $G$, if $\MembershipmemMACRoT {H (i_{1})}{x_{4}}$, then if $\MembershipmemMACRoT {H (j)}{y}$, then $\mathrm{AddCommute}$ holds for $x_{4}$ and $y$ is pairwise. Then the noncommutative product coproduct of $\mathrm{hcomm}$ is an additive monoid homomorphism of the set of functions from elements $i$ of $x_{1}$ to elements of $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {H (i)}{x_{3}})}$ and $G$ defined as the noncommutative product coproduct of the function that maps $i$ to the subtype of $H$ applied to $i$ and $\mathrm{AddSubgroup\_\_addCommute\_subtype\_of\_addCommute}$ applied to $\mathrm{hcomm}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{Pairwise} (\lambda i_{1} , j . \forall x_{4} \in G . \forall y \in G . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {H (i_{1})}{x_{4}}\rightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {H (j)}{y}\rightarrow \mathrm{AddCommute} (x_{4} , y))\vdash \operatorname{noncommPiCoprod} (\mathrm{hcomm}):= \operatorname{noncommPiCoprod} (\lambda i . \operatorname{subtype} (H (i)), \mathrm{AddSubgroup\_\_addCommute\_subtype\_of\_addCommute} (\mathrm{hcomm}))\in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (x_{1} \rightarrow \SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {H (i)}{x_{3}})}, G)$

AddMonoid.minOrderdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Order.Min, function or constant
Wording
the minimum order of x (from the docstring)
English
Let $x$ be a set. Then the minimum order of $x$ is a natural number defined as the infimum of the function that maps $a$ to the infimum of the function that maps $\mathrm{\_ha}$ to the infimum of the function that maps $\mathrm{\_ha'}$ to the additive order of $a$.

English

Mathlib.GroupTheory.PGroup

IsAddPGroupdefMathlib.GroupTheory.PGroup, predicate
Wording
p is a additive group (from the name)
English
Let $p$ be a natural number. Let $G$ be a set. Then $p$ is an additive group, if for all elements $g$ of $G$, there exists a natural number $k$, such that $\EqtMACRoH {\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {(p ^ {k})}{g}}{0}$.

Let $x_{2}$ be an additive monoid homomorphism of $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {H}{x_{3}})}$ and $A$. Then the transfer of $x_{2}$ is an additive monoid homomorphism of $G$ and $A$.
Formula
$x_{2} \in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {H}{x_{3}})}, A)\vdash \operatorname{transfer} (x_{2})\in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (G , A)$
AddSubgroup.leftTransversals.diffdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Transfer, function or constant
Wording
the difference of S and T (from the name)
English
Let $x_{2}$ be an additive monoid homomorphism of $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {H}{x_{3}})}$ and $A$. Let $S$ and $T$ be elements of $\operatorname{LeftTransversal}$ applied to $H$. Then the difference of $S$ and $T$ is an element of $A$.
Formula
$x_{2} \in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {H}{x_{3}})}, A), S , T \in \operatorname{LeftTransversal} (H)\vdash \operatorname{diff} (x_{2} , S , T)\in A$

MonoidHom.transferSylowdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Transfer, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $P$ be an element of $\mathrm{Sylow}$ applied to $p$ and $G$ such that the normalizer of $P$ is less than or equal to the centralizer of $P$. Then $\operatorname{transferSylow}$ applied to $P$ and $\mathrm{hP}$ is a monoid homomorphism of $G$ and $\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {\operatorname{toSubgroup} (P)}{x_{1}})}$ defined as the transfer of the identity map of $\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {\operatorname{toSubgroup} (P)}{x_{1}})}$.
Formula
$P \in \mathrm{Sylow} (p , G), \operatorname{normalizer} (P)\leq \operatorname{centralizer} (P)\vdash \operatorname{transferSylow} (P , \mathrm{hP}):= \operatorname{transfer} (\operatorname{id} (\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{toSubgroup} (P)}{x_{1}})}))\in \mathrm{MonoidHom} (G , \SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{toSubgroup} (P)}{x_{1}})})$

Subgroup.transferFunctiondefMathlib.GroupTheory.Transfer, function or constant
Wording
the transfer function of g and x1 (from the name)
English
Let $H$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $g$ be an element of $G$. Let $x_{1}$ be a quotient of $G$ and $H$. Then the transfer function of $g$ and $x_{1}$ is an element of $G$ defined as the product of the exponentiation of $g$ to the cast of $\operatorname{snd}$ applied to $\operatorname{quotientEquivSigmaZMod}$ applied to $H$, $g$ and $x_{1}$ and $\operatorname{out}$ applied to $\operatorname{out}$ applied to $\operatorname{fst}$ applied to $\operatorname{quotientEquivSigmaZMod}$ applied to $H$, $g$ and $x_{1}$.
Formula
$H \in \mathrm{Subgroup} (G), g \in G , x_{1} \in \operatorname{Quotient} (G , H)\vdash \operatorname{transferFunction} (H , g , x_{1}):= g ^ {\operatorname{cast} (\operatorname{snd} (\operatorname{quotientEquivSigmaZMod} (H , g , x_{1})))}\times \operatorname{out} (\operatorname{out} (\operatorname{fst} (\operatorname{quotientEquivSigmaZMod} (H , g , x_{1}))))\in G$
Subgroup.transferSetdefMathlib.GroupTheory.Transfer, function or constant
Wording
the transfer transversal as a set of H and g (from the docstring)
English
Let $H$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $g$ be an element of $G$. Then the transfer transversal as a set of $H$ and $g$ is an element of $\wp G$ defined as the range of $\operatorname{transferFunction}$ applied to $g$.
Formula
$H \in \mathrm{Subgroup} (G), g \in G \vdash \operatorname{transferSet} (H , g):= \operatorname{range} (\operatorname{transferFunction} (H , g))\in \wp G$
Subgroup.transferTransversaldefMathlib.GroupTheory.Transfer, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $H$ be a subgroup of $G$. Let $g$ be an element of $G$. Then $\operatorname{transferTransversal}$ applied to $H$ and $g$ is an element of $\operatorname{LeftTransversal}$ applied to $H$ defined as the transfer transversal as a set of $H$ and $g$.
Formula
$H \in \mathrm{Subgroup} (G), g \in G \vdash \operatorname{transferTransversal} (H , g):= \operatorname{transferSet} (H , g)\in \operatorname{LeftTransversal} (H)$
